| # | Discrepancy | How this session handles it |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | The core OCR source reproduces the owned 2018 Main Examination item with the directive **"Comment"**, while the locally held official paper prints **"Discuss"** | the PYQ section reproduces the **official** wording, records the variance explicitly, and notes that the two directives demand slightly different things |
| 2 | The core OCR source states **44** Indian World Heritage properties (**36 cultural, 7 natural, 1 mixed**) as of **September 2025**, while the canonical owner records **45** (**37 + 7 + 1**) verified **4 September 2026** | Lesson 13 prints both, identifies the earlier figure as a **pre-Sarnath photograph** rather than an error, and states the rule that a total is only as good as its date |
| 3 | The core OCR source states **15** Indian intangible-heritage items **as on August 2025**, while the canonical owner records **16** verified **4 September 2026** | Lesson 14 prints both and identifies the earlier figure as predating the **Deepavali** inscription of 10 December 2025 |
| 4 | The core OCR source's prose states that India has **14** documentary-register entries, while the table printed immediately below it contains **sixteen** rows | Lesson 15 prints the ledger as given, records the internal disagreement openly, and **asserts no total anywhere in this session** |
| 5 | The core OCR source states only that the 1958 Act prohibits construction within **100 metres**, extendable by Government, and does not print the **regulated** band or the permission architecture | Lesson 7 teaches the two-band architecture from the owner material and prints the source's compressed formulation beside it, recording the compression rather than smoothing it over |
| 6 | The property record names the Somanathapura temple **Keshava**; the core OCR source's table labels it **Chennakeshava** | Lesson 13 prints both forms and states that the variance is recorded, not resolved |
| 7 | The core OCR source prints Article 49's verb as **"spoilation"**; the standard constitutional text is **"spoliation"** | Lesson 4 records the variance and uses the standard form; nothing in the argument turns on it |
| 8 | The core OCR source states that the performing-arts academy has **three** constituent units while naming only **two**, and separately lists a longer set of institutions it has established, repeating one of them in a different form | Lesson 10 prints both statements as given and records that they are not obviously consistent; the third constituent unit is not identified and is not supplied |
| 9 | The enabling statute of the national culture fund appears as the **Charitable Endowment Act, 1890** in the core OCR source and as the **Charitable Endowments Act, 1890** in the canonical owner | Lesson 16 records the naming variance and does not resolve it |
| 10 | The core OCR source's reproduction of the owned 2024 Preliminary item differs from the official paper in the wording of its **fourth response choice** | the PYQ section records the variance without displaying either option set, since the choices are withheld under the reproduction policy |
| 11 | The core OCR source's tentative-list table still prints **Sarnath**, which the 25 July 2026 inscription has made obsolete | Lesson 13 uses this as the worked demonstration that a tentative list is a queue whose entries are worked through, and records the table as dated rather than wrong |
| 12 | The core OCR source's intangible-heritage graphic on its element page is OCR-garbled in the held copy, and its element table is clipped beyond the first row | the element ledger in Lesson 14 is taken from the canonical owner's dated ledger; the garbled graphic supplies no year, name or count in this session |
| 13 | The core OCR source's sidebars for the arts centre, the monuments authority, the historical-research council and the heritage trust carry **headings only**, with the body text present in the held copy as a linked image rather than as recoverable text | Lesson 9 records those bodies at the function level only, and the bounded material-fact pass table above declares exactly what was and was not established |
| 14 | The core OCR source presents the benefits of World Heritage status as an **uncritical list** with no corresponding costs | Lesson 12 prints the list as given and supplies the paired cost for each benefit from the advanced owner, and the remediation set treats reproducing the list alone as an error |
| 15 | The ninth documentary-register row is printed in the held copy of the core source as **Maitreyayvarakarana**, while the standard rendering of the title is **Maitreyavyakarana** | Lesson 15 records both forms explicitly and adopts neither silently |
| 16 | Several documentary-register titles carry **diacritical marks** in the core source - on the Shantinatha and Laghukalachakratantrarajatika entries in particular - which are rendered without diacritics throughout this session | Lesson 15 declares the normalisation as presentational rather than corrective; no title is otherwise altered |
| 17 | The core OCR source compresses the 1972 Act's **two-limb definition of an antiquity** into a single hundred-year rule and places manuscripts alongside paintings under it, losing the separate **seventy-five-year** threshold for a manuscript, record or other document and its accompanying value test | Lesson 6 teaches both limbs from the statutory text, records the compression explicitly, and adds a trap and a scoring deduction for stating a single threshold; the register notes, maps and Mains models are harmonised to the two-limb form |
| 18 | The core OCR source's **national leader figure** for World Heritage properties is several inscription cycles out of date | every occurrence was removed rather than replaced, because no authoritative dated national total could be confirmed; Lesson 11 states the omission openly and makes the distribution argument without any ranking |
| 19 | The core OCR source's **dated global total for the Urgent Safeguarding List** could not be corroborated, and the figures in circulation disagree with one another | no global total for that list is carried anywhere in this session; Lesson 14 states the refusal and explains why India's absence from the list remains safe without it |

## Declared gaps - material a candidate may expect that this session does not supply
